- `validate`, `config_hash` and `build_header` refuse a tag set in which a `cmd.<x>` queue has no queue named `result.<x>` of kind RESULT, or whose `result.<x>` queue does not have the consumer of `cmd.<x>` as producer and its producer as consumer. The status is `INVALID_ARGUMENT` ("tag set: invalid value"), the detail names the `cmd` queue, the result queue it needs and the two processes. The pair is found by name only.
- A `result` queue without a `cmd` queue is allowed: it costs a region and harms nothing, and the issue asks for no more.
- A `cmd` queue whose name leaves no room for the longer prefix (`cmd.` plus 28 bytes) cannot be paired and is refused by the same rule.
- The library keeps its branch for a `cmd` queue without an answer queue (the answer is dropped and counted): the header is outside input to the library. Its tests (`Queues.AnExpiredRequestWithoutAResultQueueIsDroppedAndCounted` and the `cmd.io.solo` part of conformance test 17) stay: the queue tests build a valid header with `cmd.io.solo` and `result.io.solo`, then rename the second to `result.io.sola` in the header bytes before any process attaches, as the other tests of bad header input change its bytes (lead session, review of PR #80).
Reason: the pair rule is a property of the queue table as a whole, so it belongs where the table is checked; `INVALID_ARGUMENT` keeps `NOT_FOUND` for references to processes and owners. Changing the bytes of a built header tests the branch without a second way to make a header.
Consequences:
- Contract 0.17, section 9 and 9.2; `libs/scada-tag/README.md`, "Tag-set rules", which also gains the row for one process at both ends (D-40).
- The tag sets of the tests: the attach fixture gains `result.io.web` (one region more for `io`, three cursors in a `proc` region), the queue tests gain `result.io.solo`, renamed in the header (queue ids shift), and the hash test renames an events queue instead of the result queue. Test: `TagSetValidate.RequiresTheResultQueueOfEveryCommandQueue`.

## D-53: The processes not attached at the first check of a new generation are logged; the start value of `down` is in the contract
Date: 2026-11-15
Phase / issue: 1, #60
Status: accepted

Context: since D-38 a new generation starts with `down` 1 for every process but `tagd`, so a process that never attaches is no change and left no trace in the log of `scada-tagd`. The contract did not state that start value.
Options: one record listing every process not attached; one record per process; nothing, as D-38 left it.
Decision:
- At the first check of a new generation, `GenerationHost::check` lists the processes whose region is missing or not ready (`DownReason::REGION_MISSING`) in `CheckReport.not_attached`, in process index order. `scada-tagd` logs `process not attached PROCESS="<name>"` at INFO for each, once, with its own `MESSAGE_ID` (`0f57ce3d6dbc4891abd0e55e240f04f9`, as D-47 gives every liveness record).
- A process that has a region but is not yet RUNNING has attached and is not listed. Its flag stays 1, and it is logged `process up` when it is up.
- A reused generation lists nothing: the flags and the record are from the `scada-tagd` that created it.
- The `down` flags and the `process up` and `process down` records are unchanged.
- Contract 0.18: sections 5 and 10.1 state the start value of D-38 and this report. This is earlier than P1-13, which D-38 named.
Reason: a log field holds at most 96 bytes (`LOG_MAX_VALUE_BYTES`) and a tag set may name 1,024 processes of up to 31 bytes. One list would be cut or would need to be split over several records. One record per process keeps every name and has the same form as `process up`. The list comes from the check, not from a second look at the directory: it is what that check found.
Consequences:
- A tag set of N processes that none has attached gives N INFO records at start, once.
- Tests:
  - `Host.TheFirstCheckOfANewGenerationNamesTheProcessesNotAttached`, `Host.TheFirstCheckNamesEveryProcessWhenNoneHasAttached` and `Host.AReusedGenerationNamesNoProcessNotAttached`;
  - `Host.AProcessThatAttachesBeforeTheFirstCheckIsReportedUp` also checks the list;
  - `ReportLog.EachProcessNotAttachedIsOneInfoRecord` (level, `MESSAGE_ID`, order);
  - `TagdService.AProcessNotAttachedAtTheFirstCheckIsLogged` fails without the change.

## D-54: Queues in a RETIRING generation: posts refused, a seq_cst handshake, drains that answer ABORTED
Date: 2026-11-16
Phase / issue: 1, #65 (P1-09b, the second pull request of P1-09)
Status: accepted; the design was decided by the committee of the lead session on 2026-11-15 (comments on #17, scope of #65)

Context: contract 11.3 asked a producer to stop posting once it saw RETIRING, as a duty of the process. A producer can post for up to a third of its heartbeat timeout after RETIRING, after the consumer's drain, and the record is accepted with `SCADA_OK` and then lost: an action without an audit record.
Options: leave it to the processes; let the library refuse posts in a RETIRING generation, with an ordering that makes every accepted post visible to the final drain.
Decision: the library refuses (contract 9.5).
- `scada_queue_post` returns `SCADA_E_RETIRING` on EVENTS and CMD queues in a RETIRING generation; RESULT posts are accepted (the library's ABORTED and EXPIRED answers, a driver's answer to a request it already took).
- Handshake: EVENTS loads the state before and after publishing; the `write_seq` store, both state loads, the RETIRING store of `scada-tagd` and the consumer's state and `write_seq` loads in `scada_queue_next` are seq_cst, with no fences. An event post that returned `SCADA_OK` is then always drained; one refused after publishing may still be delivered (a journal record of an action not taken, the safe side). CMD checks before publishing only, so that a requester is never told "refused" for a request a driver may execute.
- `scada_queue_next` in a RETIRING generation never waits: EVENTS and RESULT return what is left and then `SCADA_E_RETIRING`; CMD answers every request still queued ABORTED and returns `SCADA_E_RETIRING`. Detach in a RETIRING generation does the same for every request queue the process consumes. `scada_queue_post` stays real-time safe: two more loads of the state and the out-of-line calls of the handshake (about three plain calls, a few ns); no lock, system call or allocation.
Reason: a post that the library accepts after the consumer's last look is exactly the loss the "no audit, no action" rule forbids. The seq_cst order is the cheapest that closes it without a lock in the real-time post.
Consequences:
- Tests: `Queues.InARetiringGenerationOnlyAnswersArePosted`, `AConsumerDrainsARetiringQueueWithoutWaiting`, `RequestsLeftInARetiringGenerationAreAnsweredAborted`, `ADetachInARetiringGenerationAnswersItsRequestsAborted`, and the stress `EveryPostThatReturnedOkIsDrainedAfterRetire` (200 rounds of a poster against a consumer that retires and drains; it fails in every run when the second state load is removed).
- Contract 0.19: section 9.5 (new), 9.3 (the limit of a generation change), 11.3 step 2. The layout version stays 1.
- A drain looks at once for a producer region it has not mapped (`look_now`) before it can end. Found by the conformance test below: a consumer that attached before its producer, and whose generation retired within the second before its next look, drained nothing and lost every event a post had accepted.
- The library keeps no table of pending requests and no memory of ids, so those duties are the processes' (contract 9.5): a requester clears its pending table at detach and reports a request taken and not answered as failed with outcome unknown, never as ABORTED; a consumer keys remembered ids by generation; a driver never answers in the new generation a request taken in the old one.
- `scada-tagd` counts, when it removes or abandons a retired generation, the records still in each queue (`write_seq` minus the consumer's cursor) and logs one WARNING per queue: `audit records left unconsumed in a removed generation` for an events queue, `records left unconsumed in a removed generation` for a request or result queue. `RECORDS` is `unknown` when the queue file or the cursor cannot be read, the pair is not valid, or the generation was abandoned; a generation whose header cannot be read gives one record with `QUEUE` and `RECORDS` `unknown` (`Keeper.AGenerationWithoutAReadableHeaderIsReportedUnknown`, `ReportLog.AGenerationWithoutAHeaderGivesOneUnknownRecord`). Events a consumer never drained show there, so they are not counted again at detach.
- Committee review of PR #82 (C++, SCADA architecture, Linux; merge with conditions):
  - B1: the handshake is in the model check (tests/model): `handshake.litmus` for herd7, a post against the RETIRING store and a drain, with the loss as its forbidden condition, and six copies with one access made plain (three needed on Armv8, three not). In C++20 the cycle that forbids the loss is the post's `write_seq` store and second load and the drain's state and `write_seq` loads; the post's first load and the RETIRING store are outside it and are kept seq_cst out of caution, not because they are needed; its accesses moved into four functions of their own (`HeaderView::retiring`, `HeaderView::set_retiring`, `publish_write_seq`, `queue_write_seq_sc`) that `code_shape.py` checks in the ARM64 library, with six mutants. On Armv8.0 the code tells relaxed from seq_cst, not acquire from seq_cst; the call sites are checked by review and the tests. Known limit: for a queue file created anew during the retirement the litmus test covers `write_seq` only; that the drain which maps the new file after its state load sees its name and the release store of the header's `ready` field is not ordered by C++ there (on Armv8 the hardware forbids the bad outcome, and system calls are in between), and neither part checks it.
  - B2: detach looks for a producer region it has not mapped before its drain too (`Queues.ADetachAnswersRequestsOfAQueueItHadNotSeen`, which fails without it).
  - B3: on `SCADA_E_RETIRING` an event refused means the action is not taken; a request refused fails visibly as not executed; an OPERATOR command is never posted again by the service (reference 14.4: interlocks judged on the old state, a `select_request_id` of the old generation); AUTO writes are computed again by the next cycle, outputs hold or go safe until then (reference 5.5); selections do not survive a generation change.
  - B4: a driver executes and answers a request it took before it detaches, or drops it; it never acts on it after detach.
  - B5: the record of unconsumed records has MESSAGE_ID `42c8e35948cb467eb6d894d3bb0476a8`, the positions `[FIRST_SEQ, END_SEQ)`, "audit records" for an events queue, and RECORDS "unknown" when the pair cannot be read or is not valid, or the generation was abandoned; after the 30-s timeout the count is an upper bound. It is logged from `report_log.cpp` (`log_unconsumed`, tested field by field).
  - N1, N2: the C header says what SCADA_E_RETIRING of `scada_queue_next` means for RESULT, and that a drain makes system calls even with timeout 0. The look is not throttled: a consumer that gets SCADA_E_RETIRING stops calling, so it happens about once per drain.
  - N3: the stress test's consumer gives up after 10 s; the helper's pauses are pacing, said so, and its idle waits for SIGTERM with a limit.
  - N4: contract 15, test 21 reworded with its queue part; 11.3 cites reference 5.5; 9.5 states the two consequences of the handshake.
  - N5: issues #83 (command service, phase 5) and #84 (driver-interface contract, phase 2); #74 and #75 noted to cover the new record.
  - After #77 (D-49): `post_record` keeps the order first state load, refusals (the all-ones guard among them), high-water mark, record, seq_cst store of `write_seq`, second load. A drain looks at the producer's region by name also when one is mapped, and `drain_queue` drains the file in use and then a file created anew under the name, which `follow_queue` moves to (`Queues.ADrainFollowsAQueueFileCreatedAnewBeforeTheRetirement`, which fails with the old look: 0 of 2 events delivered). `poll` takes the retiring flag and calls `follow_queue`.
- More tests: `Keeper.RecordsLeftInARemovedGenerationAreCountedPerQueue`, `Keeper.EventIdsStartAtZeroAfterEachOfTwoGenerationChanges` (`write_seq` 0 for a consumer that creates its region first, ids from 0, two changes in a row), and `scada-tag-retiring-queue-integration-test` with the helper `scada-tag-test-crosser`: the queue part of conformance test 21 with real processes and a real restart of `scada-tagd` (3000 events, every one a post accepted is delivered; 400 requests, every one a driver executed was posted in the generation it was taken in).

## D-55: Model check tooling, the points left by the review of PR #54 (issue #63)
Date: 2026-11-16
Phase / issue: 1, #63
Status: proposed

Context: the committee review of pull request #54 (D-39) left eight points in issue #63, none blocking conformance test 4. Each is to be done or closed with a reason here. No C++ product code changes.
Options, stronger orderings in a reader: accept an acquire second load and `DMB ISH` for `fence_acquire`; keep the failure. Options, herd7 cache key: a hash of a separate build script; a hash of `action.yml`; a version number bumped by hand. Options, `apt-get` retries: apt's own `Acquire::Retries`; a shell loop; both. Options, `[clone .cold]` on real output: keep the synthetic test and record the gap; a fixture of real GCC output of test code.
Decision:
- Point 1, done: `code_shape.py` fails a read path that loads shared memory before its first acquire load ("a load of shared memory before the acquire load"). The caller's memory (the cursor) may still be loaded there. New edits in `code_shape_mutants.py`: a field load before the loop of `read_record`, a slot load before the loop of `read_string_record`, an entry load before the acquire load of `read_change` (25 edits).
- Point 2, closed, the failure is kept: an acquire second load, `DMB ISH` in a reader and a fence in the repair are safe but are not the instructions the litmus tests model; accepting them would let the code drift from the model the check exists to tie it to, and would add acceptance paths to a check whose risk is false passes. A self-test now holds the failure for each reader; `tests/model/README.md` says it, with this reason.
- Point 3, done: `code_shape_mutants.py` appends `-iquote <source directory>` and `-Wno-error` at the end of the command instead of at index 1, so a launcher in front of the compiler stays where it is. `-iquote` directories are searched before `-I` directories wherever they are on the line, so the include order is unchanged. CMake 3.28 and 4.1 write neither `CMAKE_CXX_COMPILER_LAUNCHER` nor dependency options into `compile_commands.json` (Makefiles and Ninja, checked); a launcher reaches the command only as part of the compiler (`CXX="ccache g++"`).
- Point 4, done: the script takes `--jobs` (at most the number of processors); CMake passes `--jobs 4` and sets `PROCESSORS 4` on `scada-model-code-shape-mutants-test`. The test presets run tests one at a time today; the property matters for `ctest -j`.
- Point 5, done: the build's commands carry no `-MD`/`-MF` in `compile_commands.json` (see point 3), but another generator or tool may write them, and two edits of one source would then rewrite the same dependency file of the build tree at once. The script drops `-MD`, `-MMD`, `-MP` and `-MF`, `-MT`, `-MQ` with their file, separate or joined, and `-o`, `-c` and the source, then adds its own `-o` and `-c`. A self-test covers both commands.
- Point 6, done: the cache key is `herd7-<commit>-<ImageOS>-<ImageVersion>-<machine>-<recipe>`, `recipe` the first 16 hexadecimal digits of the SHA-256 of `action.yml`, which holds the packages, the build commands and the layout: any change of the file builds herd7 again, at the cost of a rebuild for an edit of its LLVM steps too. A separate script would leave the package list outside the hash, and a hand-bumped number can be forgotten. Built or restored, the step "Check herd7 and put it on PATH" compares `herd7 -version` with `<version>, Rev: <commit>` of `third-party/dependencies.json` (herdtools7 writes the commit of its source into the binary, `version-gen.sh`) and checks that `aarch64.cat` is installed, so a cache hit no longer skips the commit check.
- Point 7, done: both `apt-get` steps run with `-o Acquire::Retries=3` and in a loop of up to four attempts, 10 s, 20 s and 30 s apart, as the fetch of herdtools7; the loop runs `apt-get update` again, for an index that changed under it.
- Point 8, done: `fixtures/gcc13-cold.cpp`, a read attempt of the shape of section 6.2 in test code, compiled by GCC 13.3 with `-freorder-blocks-and-partition`, gives a real `[clone .cold]` part (`fixtures/gcc13-aarch64-O2-cold.txt`): a jump to `.text.unlikely+0x8`, a call of a function of that section, a jump back to `.text+0x4c` inside the retry loop. On it `code_shape.py` as of D-39 failed with "calls nested too deep": it walked the part as a call, and each visit started the counts of branches taken again. A jump between a function and its own split-off part now continues the same walk with the same counts. Self-tests: the fixture passes, a load in the cold part is checked with the registers of the hot part, a jump back past the copy fails. The `check` target leaves out `tests/model/fixtures`, as the other fixtures: the source is compiler input, not part of any build, so clang-tidy cannot analyse it (it is formatted with clang-format 18).
Reason: each point is a false pass, a fragile command or an unchecked cache path of the tooling of D-37 and D-39; closing it costs a few lines and a test. Point 8 showed a real defect that synthetic input did not.
Consequences:
- Results on this change: the self-test has 42 tests and passes; `code_shape.py` passes on the GCC 13.3 `release-arm64` library of main `cd393c9` (cross compiler `aarch64-linux-gnu-g++`); with those commands the three sources pass and all 25 edits are rejected; two of the three new edits (in `read_record` and `read_change`) passed the check of D-39. herd7 7.58 built with the recipe of `action.yml` prints `7.58, Rev: 1ca343e16a2038e406d1ac674e7e3a1b722b36c7`.
- The first pipeline run after this change builds herd7 again (new key).
- D-37 and D-39: the reader rule, the `[clone .cold]` walk, the edit count and the cache key are as written here.

## D-56: Tests that failed under extreme overload: each cause, and the load the suite is expected to pass under
Date: 2026-11-16
Phase / issue: 1, #73
Status: proposed

Context: with eight more test binaries in parallel on 4 cores, on top of a busy loop on every core and `ctest -j8` in a loop (load average 18 to 32), the tests listed in issue #73 failed now and then (79 runs of the #62 session). Runs for this issue on main `6ca90a3` (same machine type, load average up to 20) added three: `Retained.SigkillOfEveryProcessAtRandomPointsNeverLeavesAnUnreadableFile`, `Retained.Conformance21AValueWrittenRightBeforeTheChangeIsRestored` and `RealTimeSafety.*` ("no request arrived"). Each failure was traced to its cause before it was called a time budget. One is a defect of the library, already fixed by PR 86 (D-51); the others are in the tests and their tools.
Options for a test whose only fault is a fixed time it set itself: a longer time; a wait on a condition with a limit; leave it and record the load. Options for a time bound of the contract, or a heartbeat timeout that is the subject of the test: change it; leave it and record the load.
Decision:
- The unit race checker (`TagAccess.Conformance03A*`, `Conformance23A*`): a failure there was owner-down reads counted as torn (fixed in D-50). As the review of PR #78 asked, each reader must now read the live owner at least once, and after the last write: after `done` a reader reads on until it reads the owner live, for at most 30 s, while the writer writes the owner's heartbeat; it fails with "no read of a live owner" or "no read of the live owner after the last write". The string race also checks that this read gives the last write. `RaceChecker.*` shows that a reader with only owner-down reads fails.
- The library defect: a wait set never reported a queue whose producer created its file after the consumer opened the queue. The wait set reads only the file in use, which only `scada_queue_next` follows; `scada_queue_next` itself was not affected. In the rt test the feeder is not waited for, so on a loaded machine it attached after the probe opened `cmd.probe.feeder`, and the probe waited 30 s with the request in the queue ("no request arrived" while the feeder printed "posted"; 4 of about 660 loaded runs of the binary). PR 86 fixes it (`queue_switch_pending`, its review point B1, with tests for a wait set and its descriptor); this change does not. With PR 86 on main the same order showed one more effect, in the probe, not in the library: a consumer looks at each region of a peer on a timer of its own, so the probe's look could find the feeder's queue file and not yet its `proc` region (attach creates the `proc` region first; this entry first said the opposite, corrected by D-61); the probe then took the request and made its real-time calls within the second before its next look, judged the feeder REGION_MISSING, and its answer post got PEER_DOWN and the EXPIRED answer was dropped (`calls=13 failed=1 ... dropped=1`, 2 of 1,074 loaded runs). That is the contract's look interval (at most once a second, none in a real-time call). The probe waited, before its markers, until it saw the feeder live, calling `scada_tag_notify` meanwhile; D-61 removed that wait. The library behaviour, an answer dropped as "requester down" within about 1 s of the requester's attach, is issue #92, closed by D-61.
- Test tools that wrote no heartbeat while waiting, which an overloaded machine turned into a wait that never ends:
  - `scada-tag-rt-probe` waited up to 30 s for the feeder's request in one call and wrote no heartbeat. A feeder that posted more than the probe's timeout (3,000 ms) after the probe attached saw it down (contract 9.1) and never posted. The probe now waits in slices of 100 ms and writes its heartbeat between them. `RealTimeSafety.AProbeThatWaitsLongStaysLiveForTheFeeder` stops the feeder until the probe has waited 4 s, and fails without the change.
  - In `QueueProcesses.Conformance17*` the test process `web` has no heartbeat but the one of its attach. A driver that restarted more than 3,000 ms after it judged `web` down and dropped the ABORTED answer (contract 9.2, `answers_dropped`); the test waited 10 s for it. `web` now has a heartbeat timeout of 30 s (`LOAD_HEARTBEAT_TIMEOUT_MS`), the wait for an answer is WAIT_LIMIT, and the queue helper prints `dropped=<n>`, which the test checks is 0.
  - In the unit fixture only a test writes heartbeats. `Observer.ReportsABaseDirectoryThatWasSetUpAgain` (TAGD_DOWN) and `Queues.AWaitSetWakesForAQueue` (PEER_DOWN) now write the heartbeat right before the call, and ask again only when the time since that heartbeat is at least the timeout, which is what the contract's judgement needs. The fixture's timeouts stay 3,000 ms: about 50 tests in 10 files set heartbeats in the past against them.
- Fixed times that were only a test convenience:
  - `Retained.Sigkill*` killed after 20 to 400 ms counted from the retainer's attach (132 ms in round 0 with the fixed seed). Round 0 has no file of an earlier round, and on a loaded machine scada-tagd had not written the first one yet: "no valid retain file, round 0" (5 of 39 runs at load average 14). The kill point now counts from a valid file on disk (a wait of at most WAIT_LIMIT); later rounds find the file of the round before at once.
  - `Retained.Conformance21*` needs a value written after the first file, so that only the write right before the generation change can give the restored value; it took that from the 10 ms period of the retainer, and on a loaded machine the change came first ("not restored", `leaving last=1`; the value restored was the last one written). It now waits until an observer context reads the tag at 2 or more before it stops the first scada-tagd.
  - `Queues.TagdReportsNoBrokenQueueWhileBothSidesRun` gave 20,000 records 10 s in all; producer and consumer go in step through a small queue, and at load average 15 a run took 0.4 to 3.5 s against 0.1 s unloaded (3,634 records in 10 s in the #62 logs). The limit is now 10 s without a record moved.
  - `SimProcesses.ALargeLayoutDoesNotReadAsDownWhileItStarts` used a heartbeat timeout of 100 ms, which any stall of the machine exceeds. It is now `ALargeLayoutWritesItsHeartbeatWhileItResolves`: a held simulator of 40,000 tags must show 156 heartbeats when it prints `ready`, one every 256 tags; without them the held loop (one every 20 ms) reaches that count only about 3 s later.
- Left as they are, and recorded in `tests/README.md`, "Load": the contract's bounds (`Conformance05A*` 2,000 ms, `Conformance02*` 95 %, `ChangeRingProcesses.AKilledOwnerGivesAGapWithinItsHeartbeatTimeout`, a gap after 2,368 ms), and `TagdService.Conformance20RestartLeavesTheOtherProcessRunning`, whose short timeout of `io` (1,000 ms) is what it checks against; scada-tagd's own log showed the machine stalled 3,209 ms. The ctest limits are limits for a hung test, not bounds and not waits. `scada-tag-unit-test` takes 35 s unloaded on main `1c4f323` (6.5 s of CPU, the rest fixed waits of tests), and its limit is raised from 60 s to 120 s (lead session, 2026-11-16); splitting the binary is left for later. `scada-model-code-shape-self-test` (9.5 s of CPU) keeps 60 s; under eight more binaries in parallel it can be exceeded.
Reason: a test may depend on how fast the machine is only where the time is the subject of the test; every other dependence is a wait on a condition with a limit. The contract's bounds are the contract's.
Consequences:
- `tests/README.md`, "Load", states the load the suite is expected to pass under and what fails beyond it.
- The queue helper's attach line gains `dropped=<n>` (test tool, not installed).

## D-57: P1-12, conformance suite and performance gate: the conformance tests that were missing, and where test 26 gates on SCADA_E_BUSY
Date: 2026-11-16
Phase / issue: 1, #20
Status: accepted for the first part (tests 3, 11, 15 and 26). The plan, the split into three pull requests and the gating of test 26 were decided by the committee of the lead session (the owner delegated these decisions to it) on 2026-11-16. The benchmarks, the performance gate and the report follow in the next parts of #20 and are added here.

Context: the issue asks for every conformance test of contract section 15 automated in the pipeline on both architectures. Section 15 now has 29 tests. A survey against the tests on `main` (7ee01df) found four with a gap: test 3 did not put writers and readers on separate cores; test 11 showed a full `/dev/shm` only by a file-size limit for scada-tagd's start; test 15 had no process whose owner threads each post to their own queue; test 26 had no test at all. Test 27 (latency figures) is the benchmark part of the issue and follows with the performance gate.
Options, test 3 on a machine with one processor: skip; fail; skip unless the environment requires the processors. Options, test 11: a file-size limit; a small tmpfs mounted by the test (root); a user namespace without root. Options, the criterion of test 26: gate in every build; gate in the optimised builds and report in the others; report only.
Decision:
- Test 3 (`TagAccessProcesses.Conformance03MaximumWriteRateWithFourReaders`, CTest test `scada-tag-separate-cores-integration-test`, so that a skip shows as skipped through SKIP_REGULAR_EXPRESSION; `scada-tag-access-integration-test` leaves it out): the test pins its main thread to the first processor it may use before it starts scada-sim, which with all its owner threads inherits that one processor, then pins itself to all the others before it starts its 4 readers, and checks the simulator's set. It prints `cpus=<n>`. With fewer than 2 processors it skips, unless `SCADA_REQUIRE_SEPARATE_CORES=1`, which the `build and test` and `sanitizers` jobs set: there it fails. The helper is `scada/test/cpu_affinity.h` in `scada::test-support`. On the pipeline runners, which are virtual machines, "separate cores" means separate virtual processors.
- Test 11 (`FullShm.Conformance11AttachToAFullFileSystemFailsWithAnError`, label `root`): the test moves into a mount namespace of its own whose mounts do not propagate (`unshare(CLONE_NEWNS)`, `/` made private), mounts a 1 MiB tmpfs over its temporary directory, starts scada-tagd there and fills the file system. An owner process then ends with exit code 1 and `SCADA_E_IO`, not by a signal; an attach in the test process gets `SCADA_E_IO` with errno `ENOSPC`; with the filler removed the owner attaches. TearDown unmounts; a test binary that dies takes the namespace and the mount with it. `/dev/shm` is not touched. A user namespace would need no root, but Ubuntu 24.04 restricts unprivileged user namespaces, and the runner has sudo.
- Test 15 (`OwnerThreads.Conformance15OwnerThreadsWriteAndPostWithoutARace`) is a scaled-down form of tests 2 and 13, not the reference load of test 2: 4 owners of 256 tags, 64 rounds, so that it runs in seconds under the thread sanitizer, which is what the test is for. One test process holds the process `multi` with 4 owners, each written by its own thread (256 UINT64 tags, 64 rounds, values whose fields are derived from the round and the slot) and each posting one event per round to its own queue of 8 places, and the process `sink` with 2 readers, a ring consumer per owner that keeps up (the writer waits while it is half a ring ahead), a ring consumer per owner that does not read and must be overrun once, and a queue consumer per owner. It is a unit test, so it runs in every build and under the thread sanitizer in `sanitizers (tsan)`, which sees every thread of the one process. `sink` attaches before `multi`: a producer that attaches before its consumer judges it down until it looks for its region again, at most once a second, and refuses the posts until then (contract 11.2).
- Test 26 (`FalseTrips.Conformance26RealTimeReadersBesideTheOwnersTripNothing`, label `root`): scada-sim writes the reference load (50,000 tags, 10 owner threads, 50,000 updates per second) as a normal process with the default heartbeat timeout of 3,000 ms. The helper `scada-tag-rt-reader` attaches as a process the tag set marks REALTIME, with the default 500 ms, and runs 4 readers under SCHED_FIFO (priority 20), reader i on the i-th processor in turn, so that they share every processor with the owners. Each reader is a 10 ms control task that reads 5,000 tags per cycle with `scada_tag_read_many` of 500: 2,000,000 reads per second in all. This load is fixed and is not tuned. The helper's main thread runs under SCHED_FIFO (priority 10) and writes the heartbeat every 100 ms. The helper limits its real-time CPU time (`RLIMIT_RTTIME`, 0.5 s soft, 1 s hard) and its run time, and dies with the test binary. The run lasts 10 s, or `SCADA_CONFORMANCE_SECONDS`.
- Every build fails test 26 on a false trip or a wrong read: a read that gives OWNER_DOWN while the owner runs, `REASON="heartbeat stale"` or `heartbeat late` in the log of scada-tagd, a torn, backwards or failed read, a simulator below 95 % of the rate. The criterion of contract section 16 for the read retry limit (fewer than 1 `SCADA_E_BUSY` in 1,000,000 reads) gates only in an optimised build without a sanitizer (CMake passes `SCADA_TAG_BUSY_GATE=1` for Release and RelWithDebInfo); the other builds print the rate with its sample size.
- The gate on `SCADA_E_BUSY` is statistical: a run fails when its count is above the 99.9 % quantile of a Poisson count whose mean is what a rate of exactly 1 in 1,000,000 gives over the reads that run made (35 for 20.24 million reads, the 10 s run; 1,308 for 1.2 billion, a 600 s run). The test prints the count, the reads, the bound and the rate. A change that doubles the rate fails it most of the time; a build exactly at the criterion fails it about once in a thousand runs. A fixed limit of "fewer than 1 in 1,000,000" on a sample of 20 million would fail now and then without a change of the code (21 or more). The 10 s gate catches a regression, that is strong evidence of a rate above 1 in 1,000,000; it does not prove the criterion: it passes up to 35 in 20.24 million reads (about 1.7 per million), and to show a rate below 1 in 1,000,000 at 99.9 % confidence a run of 20.24 million reads would need 7 or fewer. The proof is a 600 s run of the perf build on both runners, by its measured rate; the second part of #20 adds it to the workflow `measurements`. The pipeline runs test 26 in the dev build (`sudo ctest --preset root`) and in the release build (step "False trips as root (release)"), on both architectures.
Reason: the criterion is a property of the code that ships, and the dev build is not optimised. What causes `SCADA_E_BUSY` here: a real-time reader that wakes on a processor where an owner thread, which is not real-time, is in the middle of a write preempts it, and the record stays odd until the reader's cycle ends (1.5 ms to 7 ms here, up to 65 ms on the container below); every reader on any processor that reads that tag meanwhile spends its 64 attempts and gets `SCADA_E_BUSY`. This is the priority inversion of a sequence lock with a real-time reader and a writer that is not real-time on one core. The unoptimised write path keeps the record odd longer. The rate is per read, so it scales with the reads per cycle, which is why the load is fixed. On a target where the real-time tasks have processors of their own it should not arise. Measured on a 4-processor x86-64 container (Linux 6.18, no real-time kernel), 10 s runs of about 20.2 million reads each: dev build, 7 runs: 31, 26, 1, 0, 0, 20, 0 (the worst 1.5 per million: 3 of 7 above the criterion); release build, 5 runs: 0, 1, 1, 0, 0 (the worst 0.05 per million). On the pipeline runners (both 4 virtual processors), release build, 10 s runs of 20.24 million reads each: run 43855572534 of PR #87 (commit 31fb21a), x86-64 0 and ARM64 0, bound 35 (longest real-time cycle 420 us and 287 us); an earlier run on 4624ad6 (43853280561, cancelled by a later push before its `check` finished) gave x86-64 0 and ARM64 13, 0.64 per million. A 10 s run can show no rate much below 0.05 per million; the 600 s runs of the perf build on both runners decide the point of section 16, once the second part of #20 adds them to the job `measurements`.
Consequences:
- New: `tests/support/include/scada/test/cpu_affinity.h` (`allowed_cpus`, `pin_this_thread`, `cpus_of`, `PinnedThread`), `libs/scada-tag/tests/owner_threads_test.cpp`, `tests/integration/full_shm_root_integration_test.cpp` (`scada-tag-full-shm-root-test`), `tests/integration/false_trip_root_integration_test.cpp` (`scada-tag-false-trip-root-test`) and its helper `tests/integration/rt_reader_main.cpp` (`scada-tag-rt-reader`, not installed).
- The pipeline sets `SCADA_REQUIRE_SEPARATE_CORES=1` in `build and test` and `sanitizers`, and runs test 26 in the release build as root.
- Results on this change, the container above: test 15 passes in the dev build (1.1 s) and the tsan build (1.3 s); test 3 passes with 4 processors, skips with 1, and fails with 1 and `SCADA_REQUIRE_SEPARATE_CORES=1`; test 11 passes 3 of 3; test 26 passes in the dev and the release build. No false trip in any of 13 runs of test 26.

D-57, second part (issue #20, benchmarks and the performance gate), decided by the committee of the lead session on 2026-11-16:
- The gate. Both `build and test` jobs build the `perf` preset and run `ctest --preset perf --verbose` in the step "Perf gate (perf)". This replaces the line of D-13 "Performance tests are not run by the pipeline" and the report-only steps of D-32 and D-41. A test fails on a bound, on x86-64 and ARM64 alike. Each bound and its source:
  - the simulator rate of 50,000 updates per second: reference 15.2 (limits of a paced run from D-41);
  - the wake-up 99th percentile of one consumer below 1 ms: the target of issue P1-07 (D-32), for contract 15 test 24;
  - the scan of 5,000 tags (`scada_tag_write_many`), median below 0.5 ms: contract section 16, "cost of clock reads in `write`". The median gates on shared machines; the report gives the 99.9th percentile beside it, which missed 0.5 ms on a shared machine (2.5 ms in one local run of the perf build); the target board (issue #41) decides the worst case;
  - the database below 64 MB: the size half of contract section 16, "default change ring capacity". Its other half, no gap for a consumer that keeps up with the reference load, is not gated: conformance tests 13 and 14 check gaps at smaller loads, and no test yet looks for gaps at the reference load;
  - scada-tagd, scada-sim and the bench process together below 1.5 GB of proportional set size: a sanity bound chosen here, not a requirement. Reference 15.2 sets 1.5 GB for all services; these are only a part of them;
  - a queue post below 10 us per record: a loose regression bound of D-49 (condition C9), not a requirement.
  Exceptions: the wake-up of 8 observers is reported only (D-42), until the committee rules on it; the 99.9th percentiles of test 27 are reported only, because on a shared virtual machine they measure the scheduling of the host.
- Regression. `scada-tag-latency-perf-test` (conformance test 27) compares the median of each call with `tests/perf/baseline/<processor>.txt` and fails above 2.0 times it (`SCADA_PERF_TOLERANCE`). The medians gate because each comes from 2,000 to 200,000 calls of one run; 2.0 because the hosted runners land on different host processors, and the regressions this path can have (a system call, an allocation, a lock, a cache line shared with a writer) cost a multiple. A call the baseline does not name is reported only. A gate must not pass without measuring: a figure that cannot be read (a missing `smaps_rollup` field, a database that cannot be walked, a simulator that never writes) fails the test; a baseline file with a line that is not a known call and a positive median fails it; and a baseline directory that holds baselines but none for the processor of the build fails it, so that a wrongly named file cannot switch the check off. No baseline directory at all is report only. The perf test preset and the step "Perf gate (perf)" fail when they find no test (`noTestsAction: error`, `--no-tests=error`). This pull request ships no baseline: the calls are reported and the bounds above gate. The baselines come in the third part of #20, from the spread of the runs in the workflow `measurements` (`latency_spread`, 10 fresh runners per architecture) and its 600 s runs, at 2.0 times, and change afterwards only in a pull request that says why.
- Test 27 under the reference load: scada-sim writes 50,000 tags at 50,000 updates per second; the bench process reads them and writes 5,000 tags of its own. Each call is timed alone with CLOCK_MONOTONIC; the time of one clock reading is printed beside the figures. Samples: 200,000 reads and writes, 20,000 calls of 500 tags, 2,000 scans.
- The workflow `measurements` (`.github/workflows/measurements.yml`) replaces the job of D-31 in `pipeline.yml`. By hand it runs on x86-64 and on ARM64 with `isb` and `yield`; every Monday at 03:17 UTC on x86-64 and ARM64 with `isb`. It runs `scripts/measure-seqlock.sh`, conformance tests 2 and 26 for 600 s each (`scripts/run-ctest-test.sh`, which runs a CTest test without its time limit; it supports a command of one program without an emulator only, and fails otherwise), and every `perf` test, in the `perf` build, and keeps the output for 90 days. It checks its inputs first (`seconds` an integer from 1 to 1,500, `runs` 5 or 10), and every step that pipes into `tee` runs under `bash` with `pipefail`, so that a failure shows. The weekly run costs the owner Actions minutes (about two jobs of 35 minutes); deleting its `schedule` or disabling the workflow stops it.
- `tests/conformance.md` lists the tests that automate each conformance test, and the unit test `scada-conformance-index` (a CMake script) fails if a test of section 15 has none, if a name in it is no test, or if a test named `Conformance<NN>...` is not in row NN.

D-57, third part (issue #20, the measurements, the baselines and the report), decided by the committee of the lead session on 2026-11-16:
- The measurements: workflow `measurements`, run 43875571560 on commit 0fd68a2 (perf build), 3 measurement jobs and 20 runs of conformance test 27, on GitHub-hosted runners with 4 vCPUs (x86-64: AMD EPYC 9V74, clock source `tsc`; ARM64: Neoverse-N2, `arch_sys_counter`). All 23 jobs passed. An earlier run, 43875565618, was cancelled within its first minute, before any test, because the workflow runs the measurement jobs also when `latency_spread` is set, and two dispatches would have run them twice. `docs/reports/phase-1.md` has every figure.
- Contract 0.22, section 16, settled by these figures: the read retry limit at 64 (conformance test 26, 600 s: 0 `SCADA_E_BUSY` in 1.20 billion reads on x86-64 and on ARM64 with the shipped `isb`; the unshipped `yield` gave 0.124 per million); the heartbeat defaults of 3,000 ms and 500 ms (tests 5 and 26, no process down, no late heartbeat); two clock reads per `write` (the scan of 5,000 tags: median 98 us and 197 us, at most 296 us at the 99.9th percentile in 23 runs, against 0.5 ms); the database size half of the ring capacity (28.8 MB against 64 MB). Still open: the gap half of the ring capacity (no test looks for gaps at the reference load) and huge pages (not measured). Every row names the run and the hardware, and that the target board (#41) is still to measure.
- Baselines: `tests/perf/baseline/x86_64.txt` and `aarch64.txt` hold the median of the medians of the 10 fresh runs per architecture; the gate fails above 2.0 times them. The worst of the 10 runs was 1.47 times its median on x86-64 (the scan; `write_many` of 500 tags 1.45 times; the two slow runs had a clock reading of 30 ns instead of 20 ns, a different or busier host) and 1.01 times on ARM64 (1.03 times from the fastest to the slowest run). 2.0 leaves 1.36 times above the worst x86-64 run seen.
- Not measured in phase 1, and so not claimed: the target board (#41), the fast-task jitter and overruns on a real-time kernel of reference 15.2, the ring gap at the reference load, huge pages, and the memory of `scadactl`.

## D-59: Gaps in retained values: retain file version 2, the age of a restored value, the restore information, and the stop of scada-tagd with a write in flight
Date: 2026-11-16
Phase / issue: 1, #72 (follow-up of P1-09, #17, D-45)
Status: accepted; decided by the committee of the lead session (C++, SCADA architecture, Linux systems) on the owner's delegation, 2026-11-16 (last comment of #72). Points 1, 2 and 3 (library) are done in the first pull request of #72 (#91); points 3 (`scada-tagd`), 4, 5 and 6 in the second, which closes the issue.

Context: PR #69 (D-45) left six gaps: the retain file held no quality or flags; a restored value had no age; a degraded restore showed only in counts and logs; a tag moved across a late attach had no test; a hung `fsync` delayed the exit of `scada-tagd`; and the timed-out branch of `RetainKeeper::finish` was not guarded by `newer_region_exists`.
Options, quality and flags: keep version 1; a version 2 in the reserved bytes of each record. Options, the age: refuse old values at attach; give the age and leave the policy to the configuration schema. Options, the restore outcome: counts only; a per-tag call; a field in shared memory. Options, a hung write at exit: detach the writer thread; bound the stop and leave with `std::_Exit`.
Decision:
1. Format version 2 (phase 1). The 6 reserved bytes of a record carry `quality` (1), reserved 0 (1), `quality_flags` (2) and reserved 0 (2); the record stays 24 fixed bytes plus the name. The header carries the `boot_id` of the writing boot, `written_boottime_ns` (CLOCK_BOOTTIME) and a flag CLOCK_SYNCED from `adjtimex(modes = 0)`, not set while it reports TIME_ERROR or STA_UNSYNC. The writer writes only version 2; the decoder reads 1 and 2, a version 1 record as UNCERTAIN with flags 0. A version 2 file is rejected as a whole when a quality is not GOOD or UNCERTAIN, a flag bit is unknown or one of NOT_INIT, OWNER_DOWN and RESTORED, or a reserved byte is not 0; the encoder refuses the same. A restored value is UNCERTAIN, never GOOD, with RESTORED plus the stored SUBSTITUTED, BLOCKED, TEST, OUT_OF_RANGE, OVERFLOW, LOCAL_TIME and TIME_UNCERTAIN; STALE and COMM_FAIL describe the live link and are not carried; the stored quality is for diagnosis only. The keeper treats a change of quality or flags as a change. MANUAL is the value of a mode tag, not a flag, and was already retained.
2. Age (data in phase 1, policy in phase 2, #89). Same boot: CLOCK_BOOTTIME now minus `written_boottime_ns`; another boot: CLOCK_REALTIME now minus `written_time_ns`, only if synchronised at the write and now and not negative; otherwise unknown. Attach never refuses a value on age. Phase 2 adds an optional per-tag `retain_max_age` (older or unknown: BAD + RESTORED, value kept; refused on mode, authority, counter and shelving tags).
3. Degraded restore. Library: `scada_tag_retain_info(ctx, handle, out)`, process-local, not real-time, filled at attach: origin (none, own FINAL file, own periodic file, other owner), `written_time_ns`, the age or unknown, the stored quality, whether the FINAL wait ran out for that tag. The counts of `scada_tag_retain_status` stay; the shared-memory layout does not change. `scada-tagd`: a MESSAGE_ID of its own for each retain problem record (second pull request). Phases 5 and 6 specify what `scada-control`, `scada-cmd` and `scada-io` do after a degraded restore; `scada-control` needs another owner's outcome (#90).
4. Tests of a tag moved across a late attach (second pull request).
5. A write in flight at the stop: the writer thread is not detached; at the 5 s bound the check report says so, `scada-tagd` logs at ERROR with its own MESSAGE_ID ("values unsaved at exit"), flushes the log and calls `std::_Exit` with a distinct status; the coding standard allows `_Exit` only in `main` after a bounded stop failed; `TimeoutStopSec=15s`, `KillMode=mixed` (second pull request).
6. The timed-out branch of `RetainKeeper::finish` checks `newer_region_exists` (second pull request).
Reason: version 2 fits in bytes that were reserved, so every offset stays and version 1 stays readable. No clock measures age across a reboot without time synchronisation, and refusing a value at attach would lose the last decision on modes and authority, so phase 1 gives the data and leaves the bound to configuration. A per-tag call reports a degraded restore without a change of the shared-memory layout. A detached writer would use descriptors already closed.
Consequences (first pull request):
- Layout choices left open by the decision: the header of version 2 is the 80 bytes of version 1 with CLOCK_SYNCED as bit 1 of the flags, then `boot_id` at offset 80 (16 bytes, parsed from the 36 characters of `/proc/sys/kernel/random/boot_id`; all zero when it cannot be read) and `written_boottime_ns` at 96; records start at 104. A file of 100 tags with names of 32 bytes takes 5,708 bytes (24 more than in version 1). Two boot ids that are all zero are never the same boot, so a version 1 file has an unknown age unless its clock flag says otherwise, which version 1 cannot.
- A record read with RESTORED whose value and source time are those on file is no change: the file keeps the quality and flags the owner stored, and the restore does not overwrite GOOD with UNCERTAIN after a second restart without a write (`Keeper.ARestoredValueNotWrittenSinceKeepsTheQualityOnFile`, which fails without the rule). RESTORED and OWNER_DOWN are stripped from what the keeper stores; NOT_INIT never reaches it.
- `scada_retain_status_t` grows from 16 to 24 bytes with `ages_unknown` and a reserved field (proposed to the lead with the split; contract migration note). `scada_tag_retain_info_t` is 24 bytes. A tag of another process and every tag of an observer give `SCADA_E_NOT_OWNER`; a tag of the caller not restored at this attach (no RETAIN, region reused, nothing on file) gives origin none. The output is zeroed on an error.
- Committee review of #91: B1, a record is taken as the unchanged restore only if it is exactly what the restore wrote: UNCERTAIN, RESTORED plus the carried flags of the file, the value and source time on file; an owner may store RESTORED itself, and a write that sets GOOD or a flag while keeping RESTORED is a change (`KeeperAfterRestore.AWriteThatKeepsRestoredButSetsGoodIsAChange`, `...SetsAFlagIsAChange`). B2, a rewrite does not restamp values the owner has not written: the keeper keeps per owner the stamp of the file each value came from (`OwnerState::not_written`), removed when the owner writes the tag, and a file holding any such value carries `oldest_stamp` of them: in one boot the earliest times, across boots no boot id and the earliest wall-clock time, CLOCK_SYNCED only if every source had it. A restored value the keeper has no source for has an unknown age. Tests: the rewrite at start, a later periodic file after a restore without a write, and `RetainAge.TheOldestStamp*`. The live values of a file that still holds old ones look older than they are; that is the safe side for an age bound (#89).
- `scada_tag_retain_status` takes no size and so cannot refuse a caller of the 16-byte structure; safe only because `libscada-tag` is built static (`cmake/ScadaTargets.cmake`) and its callers are rebuilt with it. Add a size argument before it is ever built shared.
- `Attachment::retain_infos_` holds one entry per handle: a second restore of the same owner replaces its entries.
- `encode_retain_file_v1` exists for the tests and the fuzz target only: the fuzz target checks that a version 1 input encodes back to its bytes as version 1 and to the same values as version 2. Six version 2 seeds were added (`four-values-v2`, `empty-v2`, `not-final-v2`, `bad-crc-v2`, `truncated-v2`, `restored-flag-v2`).
- The integration test of conformance 21 now expects flags 3072 (RESTORED and LOCAL_TIME): the retainer writes without a device time, so the library set LOCAL_TIME, which a restored value now keeps.
- Tests: `RetainFile.*` (round trip of every field, version 1 read as UNCERTAIN without flags, the writer writes version 2, every flipped bit and every truncation of both versions, damage behind a valid check sum for both, a version 2 header cut to 80 bytes, the refusals of both encoders, the carried flags), `RetainAge.*` (same boot, another boot with and without a synchronised clock, a negative difference, unknown boot ids, the clock of this boot), `RetainDirectory.LoadKeepsQualityFlagsAndTheMarkOfTheFile`, `Restore.*` (flags carried and never GOOD, every carried flag, a version 1 file, origin, write time and age in the same boot, unknown age in another boot without synchronisation, a moved tag, a reused region, the C interface and its errors), `RestoreAfterChange.AnExpiredWaitIsToldForEachTagThatNeededTheFile` and `AFinalFileThatCameInTimeIsNoExpiredWait`, `Keeper.AChangeOfQualityOrFlagsAloneIsWritten`.
- New suppression: `performance-enum-size` on the C enumeration of the origins, as for the other C enumerations of `scada_tag.h`.
Consequences (second pull request):
- Point 3, `scada-tagd`: `RetainProblem` gets a kind, and each kind has a record and a MESSAGE_ID: `retain file ignored` (`586ec48106f748609721eef8957c56d5`), `retain file not written` (`974151cda6fa452a998a576b2bb09bce`), `retain writer did not finish within its bound` (`0031dfe3391142b0b9fa995b07115e10`, ERROR), `values unsaved at exit` (`7cf5c1c8535f44f6a297db538666d7a3`, ERROR), `the retain directory is not usable` at the start (`8df1e064b8464d59ad5fa42e9a3070e4`, CRITICAL; `GenerationHost::start` reports the cause as `HostStartFailure::RETAIN_DIRECTORY`), `process still attached to a retired generation` (`349b6814c6cc4a2fafa5d3d1af16e7a7`). The common record `retained values` is gone. All in `report_log.cpp`, tested field by field; a test checks that every MESSAGE_ID of `scada-tagd` is distinct.
- Point 5: `CheckReport.retain_writer_bound_reached`. At a stop with it set, `scada-tagd` logs `values unsaved at exit`, flushes the log (at most 2 s), and calls `RunHooks::exit_now(EXIT_UNSAVED)` (3); the real `main` passes a call of `std::_Exit`, and the coding standard (section 7) allows it only there. A null hook returns 3 and joins the writer. `RunHooks::retain_file_ops` is the seam of the test; `HostOptions::retain_file_ops` carries it to the writer. Drop-in `scada-tagd.service.d/stop.conf`: `TimeoutStopSec=15s`, `KillMode=mixed`, checked by the install layout test. Test: `HungWrite.AtTheStopTagdEndsWithCode3AndTheOldFileStays`, with `scada-tagd-test-hung-writer` (the real service with an fsync that blocks): exit code 3 within 14 s of SIGTERM (about 5 s; the worst case of a stop is 12 s: a SIGTERM during the 5-s wait of the start in `finish_now`, the 5-s bound of the stop, 2 s for the log), both ERROR records with their IDs, the old file intact, and the temporary file removed by the next start.
- Point 6: `RetainKeeper::finish` checks `newer_region_exists` in the timed-out branch. `Keeper.AtTheTimeoutAHungOwnerNeverOverwritesTheValueOfANewerGeneration` writes 5 over 6 without the fix.
- Point 4, unit tests: `Keeper.AMovedTagAttachedAfterTheOldGenerationWasRemovedTakesItsFinal` (a), `Keeper.AMovedTagWhoseFormerOwnerStaysTakesThePeriodicFileAfterTheWait` (b; `final_waits_expired` 1 and the per-tag mark), `Keeper.TheNewOwnersFileIsWrittenBeforeTheFormerOwnersFileIsRemoved` (c). Integration: `MovedTag.AfterTheOldGenerationIsRemovedTheLastValueIsRestored` (the second write, io's FINAL) and `MovedTag.WhileTheFormerOwnerHangsTheWaitRunsOutAndTheFirstValueIsRestored` (io stopped with SIGSTOP; the first write, the value of the stop of the first `scada-tagd`), with real processes and a retain interval of an hour.
- Two defects found by the point 4 tests, reported to the lead and fixed here: (1) once the old generation was removed, the file of the former owner, in no generation any more, was unlinked while the new owner had not attached and had no file, so a late attach restored nothing: the check of the removal treated an owner without values as having its file. Now, once no generation is retired, an owner of `current` without a region takes from other owners' files the values of its RETAIN tags its own file lacks, through `load_retained`, the function attach uses, so both follow one precedence rule, and with the quality, flags and source time of the file they came from; once per start of `scada-tagd`, which is enough because a generation changes only at its start (D-27, D-54), so no tag moves while it runs; the owner is written in that check; the removal follows at a later check (test (a) fails without it). (2) At start the keeper filled the state of a current owner from other owners' files, and wrote a file of its own for it at the first check from the former owner's periodic file; that own file then took precedence over the FINAL file written later, so the late attach restored the older value. The start now reads the own files only for the current generation (`MovedTag.AfterTheOldGenerationIsRemovedTheLastValueIsRestored` fails without it: 5 restored instead of 6). `Keeper.FilesOfOwnersThatAreGoneAreRemovedOnceEveryOwnerHasOne` (D-45) passes unchanged. Conditions of the lead, tested in `MovedKeeper.TakenValuesKeepTheirFileAndFollowTheRuleOfAttach`: the value taken is the one attach takes from the same files, keeps its quality, flags and source time, and only RETAIN tags of the owner are taken.
- The conformance 25 integration test now looks for `retain file ignored` and its MESSAGE_ID.
- Committee review of #93:
  - B3: a value taken from another owner's file keeps that origin. The record flags byte of version 2 (offset 3, reserved before) carries ADOPTED (bit 0) and SOURCE_NOT_FINAL (bit 1, only with ADOPTED); the decoder refuses other bits and SOURCE_NOT_FINAL alone, the encoder refuses the same, version 1 cannot hold them. The keeper sets them for a value it takes (`adopt_moved_values`) and for a value the owner restored from another file, found again in the files the restore read (`note_restores`; a source no longer found gives an unknown age and SOURCE_NOT_FINAL, the safe side). Attach reports ADOPTED as origin OTHER_OWNER and SOURCE_NOT_FINAL as `final_wait_expired`; a write of the owner clears both, and the stamp follows B2. Seed `adopted-v2`. Tests: `MovedKeeper.*` (the origin after adoption and after a direct restore, the FINAL mark, the mark cleared by a write), `RetainFile.EncodeRefusesSourceNotFinalWithoutAdopted`, the damage table.
  - B4: STALE and COMM_FAIL are not stored or compared: the keeper keeps the carried flags only, so a flapping link writes nothing (`Keeper.AFlappingLinkWritesNothing`). Files written by #91 with STALE stay readable and are rewritten once without it.
  - B5: `LoadedValues::complete` is false when the directory could not be listed or held more than MAX_OWNERS other files; the adoption is then not marked done, is tried at every check, no file of a gone owner is removed, and `retain files not all read for moved tags` (ERROR, `624f9b58ba224d5eadd528279c0f8e7d`) is logged once (`MovedKeeper.MoreFilesThanAreReadKeepTheFilesOfGoneOwners`, which fails without it). Attach reads the same at most 1,023 files, as D-45 says.
  - N2: an `fsync` stuck in the kernel leaves a zombie after `_Exit` that holds the lock of the base directory; systemd's SIGKILL cannot end it, the restart fails on the lock and runs into the start limit, so `scada-tagd` stays down until the storage recovers, and an operator runs `systemctl reset-failed` and starts it (README). The stuck process cannot become a second writer. The test seam blocks in user space and does not show this.
  - N3: `adjtimex` is in the `@clock` set of systemd: `ProtectClock=yes` or `SystemCallFilter=~@clock` would make every cross-boot age unknown or end the process with SIGSYS; the unit template and the README say so.
  - N4: the README lists every MESSAGE_ID, the two queue ones included. The distinctness test keeps a hand-written list: reading `report_log.h` at test time needs a source path the unit tests do not have.
  - N7: one `values unsaved at exit` record at a stop, with `UNSAVED_OWNERS` and, when a write was in flight, `SUBJECT` the owner being written and `KEPT_WRITTEN_TIME_NS` of its file that stays (a log value holds at most 96 bytes, so they are fields of their own); `stop_unsaved` no longer logs one.
  - N8: test 21 names the carried flags, test 25 the MESSAGE_ID of its record.
  - N10: `MovedKeeper.TheStartTakesNoValueFromAFormerOwnersPeriodicFile` fails when the start reads other owners' files.
- Pipeline of #93 (head 2a15074): in asan-ubsan, `HungWrite` failed on a race in the test, not in the code: the scada-tagd started after the hung one writes io's current values at its first check, so the test now checks the old file before that start and, after it, only that the temporary file of the hung process is gone. Under ThreadSanitizer `scada-tag-unit-test` reached the 60-s limit of that time (this work adds about 7 s, mostly bounded 2-s waits for FINAL files); D-56 has since made its limit 120 s.
- Delta review of #93 (df17d43), by the C++, SCADA and Linux reviewers:
  - D1: any read error of a file but a missing one (an open failing with EMFILE, ENFILE, EIO or ELOOP, a damaged file), the own file included, makes the look incomplete, and so does a directory with more than 65,536 entries (`list_directory` now reports it). A damaged file of a gone owner then blocks the removal of gone owners' files until it is removed; the README names the operator step. Tests: `RetainDirectory.AnOtherFileThatCannotBeOpenedMakesTheLookIncomplete`, `MovedKeeper.AFileThatCannotBeOpenedKeepsTheFilesOfGoneOwners` (a symbolic link, which `O_NOFOLLOW` refuses with ELOOP); both fail without the fix.
  - D2: nothing is taken for an owner whose look was incomplete; an older value seen before the cut would stay for good. `MovedKeeper.AnOlderValueBeforeTheCutIsNotTaken` (the newest FINAL holder sorts after 1,023 other files) fails without it.
  - D3: attach reports `final_wait_expired` also for a value it takes from another owner's file that is not FINAL, whether a wait happened or not (#90). `Restore.AMovedTagFromAFileThatIsNotFinalReportsAnExpiredWait`.
  - D4: `KEPT_WRITTEN_TIME_NS` comes from memory (`OwnerState::written_time_on_disk`, from the file loaded or the last file written), never from the disk at the stop, which would block on the hanging storage. It is the `written_time_ns` of that file, which per B2 is the oldest stamp of its values and can be older than the write. The integration test checks it against the old file.
  - E1: an incomplete look is tried again once per retain interval, not at every check.
  - E2: `UNSAVED_COUNT`, and `UNSAVED_OWNERS` with as many names as fit 88 bytes, then `...`.
  - E3: kept as it is, on the safe side: a record with RESTORED and another value or source time is taken as a restore, looked up in the files, and marked ADOPTED with SOURCE_NOT_FINAL and an unknown age if no file holds it. Contract 12 now says so, with the case of an owner that writes RESTORED itself: `scada-link` on a master passes a slave's quality and flags through (reference 17; the review named reference 18, which is the phased build plan).
  - E4: accepted cost of B2: a tag the owner never writes keeps the old stamp on its whole file, so at the next start every value of that owner, also those written live, reports that old age, or an unknown one. That is the safe side for an age bound (#89).
  - E5: README: a process stuck in the kernel in state D is never reaped, systemd never sees exit code 3, only a reboot clears it; with more than 1,023 other files the adoption never completes until the files of owners in no tag set are removed.
  - E6: a tag demoted to BAD + COMM_FAIL and back costs two rewrites per flap (D-45 removes a BAD record), with no lower limit for RETAIN_NOW: issue #96, phase 1.

## D-61: A look that maps a queue file also looks at its producer's `proc` region
Date: 2026-11-16
Phase / issue: 1, #92
Status: accepted. Option B, with the creation order of option A written into the contract, was decided by the committee of the lead session (the owner delegated these decisions to it) on 2026-11-16.

Context: a consumer could take a request and drop its answer as "requester down" within about 1 s of the requester's attach (2 of 1,074 loaded runs of `RealTimeSafety.*`, D-56). The cause is not the order of creation, as the issue first said: attach already creates `proc.<process>` before the process's queue files, each complete before the rename that publishes it. It is that a consumer looks at each region of a peer on a timer of its own (`PeerRegions::refresh`, at most once a second for each slot), so its look at the requester's `cmd` queue can come up to a second before its look at the requester's `proc` region. In that time the consumer sees requests and judges the requester REGION_MISSING; its answers are refused (a driver's own result post, which is real-time and cannot look) or dropped and counted (EXPIRED, ABORTED). For a first command posted t s after the requester's attach the chance is about (1 − t²)/2 when the driver attached first: half of the starts of a command source that sends at once. A requester that restarts in the same generation reuses its files, and the driver keeps its mappings: no window.
Options: (A) create the `proc` region before the queue files, already the case and not enough alone; (B) couple the looks; (C) look again at once on REGION_MISSING when an answer is posted, possible only outside real-time paths, so not for the driver's own post; (D) write the window into the contract and leave every command service to handle "executed, answer lost".
Decision:
- (B). When a look publishes a new mapping of a queue slot this process consumes (its first file, or a file created anew, also a kept mapping used again), it looks at once at the producer's `proc` slot in the same look, under the peers' lock, and asks for that slot first if nobody has (`PeerRegions::look_at_producer`). Nothing on a real-time path: looks run in `scada_queue_open`, `scada_tag_notify`, `scada_tag_status`, the waits of `scada_queue_next` and wait sets, and the drains.
- The slot is asked for at the look that finds the queue's file, not when the queue is opened. An ask at the open, while the producer had not attached, made `OwnerThreads.Conformance15*` read its owner down for up to a second: an ask did not look again at a slot asked for before, so the resolve that followed found nothing (fixed too, below).
- The producer side: a requester judges its driver live when it posts (section 9.1). A requester that attached before its driver gets `SCADA_E_PEER_DOWN` until its next look at the driver's region (11.2), at most 1 s after the driver's attach for a process that calls `scada_tag_notify` at least once a second: a refusal, nothing put in the queue, so no outcome is lost. A requester that consumes the answer queue gets the same coupling when a look maps that queue's file. A post is real-time and cannot look, so the refusal window stays; section 9.1 states it.
- Contract 0.23: section 9.2 states the guarantee, "a process that can see a record of a queue can also see the region of the process at its other end, unless that process is down", and that an answer to a requester that is down is dropped and counted as before; section 9.1 the producer's window; section 11.2 step 3 the order (`proc` region first, each complete before its rename), relied on; section 11.2's last paragraph the exception to the once-a-second look.
- An ask (`want`) of a slot that was asked for before and is still missing looks again at once, as the first ask does (review of PR #95). Without it a `proc` slot first asked for by a coupled look while its file was missing, whose producer then attached again with a new `proc` region and its queue file kept (no new queue mapping, so no coupled look), was not looked at by a later resolve until the next `refresh`, up to a second later. Its cost is one `fstatat` per ask of a missing region: a resolve of many tags of an owner that has not attached makes one per tag.
- The exception that stays (section 9.2): a producer whose `proc` region is created anew inside a generation (removed or damaged) while its queue file is kept is read as down by the consumer until its next look at that region, at most 1 s after the producer's attach for a consumer that looks at least once a second. No queue file changes, so no coupled look happens; closing it would mean a look of the `proc` region at every look of the queue, which the committee did not ask for. In a new generation, and after a restart that reuses the files, there is no such window.
- A slot is marked asked for only after it is in the list of `refresh` (the push first), so that a failed allocation cannot leave a slot asked for that `refresh` never looks at.
- D-56 and issue #92 are corrected: the order of creation was never the cause.
Reason: the coupling closes the window for every answer, the driver's own real-time post included, for one more look per new queue file outside real-time code, and gives a guarantee a command service can rely on; (C) leaves the main path open and (D) would make "outcome unknown" a normal case of an operator command.
Consequences:
- `libs/scada-tag/src/peer_regions.cpp`: `look_at_producer`; `refresh_locked` walks the asked slots by position, since a look can ask for one.
- Tests: `Peers.AQueueFoundLaterIsSeenWithItsProducer` (the producer's slot asked for first and looked at, missing, a second before the queue's, so that only the queue's slot is due), `Peers.AQueueCreatedAnewIsSeenWithItsNewProducer` (both files created anew, the path of D-49), `Queues.AConsumerThatSeesAQueueSeesItsProducer` (the producer attaches after the open; the look of the queue maps its `proc` region). Each fails without the change. From the review: `Peers.AnAskForARegionStillMissingLooksAgainAtOnce` (fails without the look of an ask of a missing slot) and `Peers.AProducerRegionMissingAtTheCoupledLookIsFoundLater` (the coupled look finds no `proc` file; the next `refresh` finds it).
- The rt probe no longer waits until it sees the feeder live before its markers (D-56 added that wait): without the wait it passed 1,273 loaded runs of `scada-tag-rt-integration-test` (6 loops of it and 2 of unit tests, busy loops and `ctest -j8`, load average up to 22.7), where it had failed 2 of 1,074 without the coupling.

## D-62: A minimum interval between two writes of one owner's retain file
Date: 2026-11-16
Phase / issue: 1, #96 (follow-up E6 of the review of #93, D-59)
Status: accepted; decided by the committee of the lead session on 2026-11-16 (option A of the proposal, B not taken); amended the same day after the committee review of PR #111 (B1 to B4, N1 to N4): checks are counted instead of ns, with a write budget W; and after the delta check (F1 to F3, W1 to W3): each file measured on its own result, no early check, the conditions of the bound stated.

Context: a RETAIN_NOW change is written at the next check, period P = min(`heartbeat_check_ms`, the timeout of `proc.tagd` / 3, the watchdog interval / 4), 250 ms by default, and a record the owner marks BAD leaves the file at the next write (D-45). A RETAIN_NOW tag that flaps BAD with COMM_FAIL and back therefore rewrote its owner's file at every check: 60 s / P files a minute, 240 at 250 ms and 1,200 at 50 ms. A tag without RETAIN_NOW is already limited to one write per retain interval (12 a minute at 5 s). Since D-59 (B4) COMM_FAIL alone writes nothing.
Options: A, a minimum interval between two writes of one owner's file, also for RETAIN_NOW; B, a hold-off H before a BAD record removes the value from the file; both.
Decision: A alone, as a number of checks.
- A file of an owner is handed to the writer again only once K checks have passed since the last one (`checks_between_writes`), K = max(1, floor((1000 ms − W) / P) − 1), with the write budget W = 200 ms (`RETAIN_WRITE_BUDGET_NS`). At P = 250 ms K = 2 (at most every 500 ms); at 50 ms K = 15 (750 ms); at 400 ms K = 1. The first change after a quiet time (K checks or more since the last write) is handed over at the next check, as before. The gate applies to every write of a check, periodic and RETAIN_NOW; three writes are not held back: the FINAL file of an owner that left a retired generation, the write of what can be read at the timeout of a retired generation, and the flush at a stop.
- Why checks, not ns (B1 of the review): the first form, M = 1 s − P compared with the time since the last submit, missed its own bound. The loop keeps its schedule but reads `now` after it wakes, so a check is late by a varying amount less than P; when the check that wrote was later than the one M after it, the time since fell just short of M and the write slipped a whole period: a change 1 ms after a write at a check 1 ms late was handed over at the 1,000 ms check and on disk after more than 1 s. And the time of the write itself was not in the budget. Counted checks do not depend on when a check wakes: a change is handed over within K · P plus less than one period of lateness, which is (K + 1) · P ≤ 1000 ms − W (800 ms at every P the builder allows), and is on disk within 1 s while the write takes at most W.
- W is a stated condition, not a guarantee, and one budget: from the hand-over of a file to the end of its own write, including the writes of all owners queued before it (the writer is one thread for all owners), at most W. The bound has two more conditions (F3 of the delta check): no check runs longer than P, since a check that does moves the schedule of the ones after it; and the time from the start of a check (`now`) to its hand-over of the file is part of the check, not measured apart. `scada-tagd` measures each RETAIN_NOW change from the check before the one that saw it (the earliest the change can have happened) to the end of the write that carried it, by the clock of the checks up to the hand-over and by the writer's clock after, and logs `retain_now change reached the disk late` (WARNING, its own MESSAGE_ID) once per owner when it exceeds 1 s, again only after a later change was within 1 s (B3). Each file handed to the writer is measured on its own result (F1): while one write is still in flight, the next is handed over and measured from its own change; a file the writer replaced before it began carries its change into the next. A write that hangs in `fsync` raises the warning only when it ends; the WRITER_BOUND record covers a hang at a stop. When the times of consecutive changes alternate around 1 s, the once-per-owner latch resets at every other write and the warning repeats, at most once per K checks per owner, and journald's rate limit caps it.
- A check never comes early (F2 of the delta check): a wait of the loop that another signal interrupts (EINTR, for example after SIGSTOP and SIGCONT) is waited again until the scheduled time (`wait_until`). Before, it ran the check at once, which used up the slot of the next check (`next = max(next + P, done)`) and broke K · P + P (997 ms to the hand-over at 250 ms in the reviewer's probe). The warning stays as the safety net.
- K is computed from the real P: `scada-tagd` gives its loop period to the host (`GenerationHost::set_check_period`); until then the `heartbeat_check_ms` of the tag set, which bounds it from above. A period that is not positive is taken as 1 ns (precondition: period > 0); K is never below 1.
- The tag-set builder refuses a `heartbeat_check_ms` above 400 ms (`MAX_RETAIN_NOW_CHECK_MS`) in a set with a RETAIN_NOW tag: K ≥ 1 needs P ≤ (1000 ms − W) / 2. The loop checks at most every `heartbeat_check_ms`; the other limits of its period only shorten it. Before, such a set broke the 1 s bound without a word. The phase 2 configuration schema must carry this limit with its description (noted in issue #98).
- B, considered and not taken: it would relax the D-45 rule that a value the owner disowned does not come back after a restart (within H a restart would restore the last good value), a committee decision, for a gain only on tags that are already limited to one write per retain interval.
Reason: A alone bounds the worst case for every tag and keeps the 1 s bound, now with its condition stated, and D-45.
Consequences:
- Worst case per owner file, all its tags together: 60 s / (K · P) writes a minute, on average over time (a check that comes late is followed by one that may come early, and missed checks are not made up): 120 at the default check (500 ms), 80 at 50 ms (750 ms), 150 at 400 ms. With a file of 100 tags of 32-byte names (5,708 bytes, D-59), at the default check: 172,800 writes and 986 MB of payload a day; at 50 ms 115,200 writes and 658 MB; at 400 ms 216,000 writes and 1.23 GB. Before the rule: 240 a minute and about 2 GB a day at 250 ms, 1,200 a minute and 9.9 GB at 50 ms. Each write is more than its payload at the device: two 4 KiB data blocks for this file, plus two journal commits (the file's `fsync` and the directory's), each with its descriptor and the directory, inode, block bitmap and group descriptor blocks: about 6 to 12 times the payload at the file system (W3 of the delta check), and more at an eMMC, whose erase blocks and write amplification multiply it again. For one file at the default check that is about 6 to 12 GB a day at the file system.
- For N owners that all flap: writes a day = N × 86,400 s / (K · P), bytes a day = the sum of N such files. For N = 30 files of 5,708 bytes at the default check: 5.2 million writes and 29.6 GB of payload a day, about 180 to 355 GB at the file system and more at the eMMC, and all 30 queued writes must then finish within W = 200 ms, about 6.7 ms each. A RETAIN_NOW tag is for modes and authority, which do not flap in normal operation; a device tag that flaps is normally not RETAIN_NOW and writes at most once per retain interval.
- The D-45 window grows from one check to up to K · P: a BAD record leaves the file at the next write the gate allows, so a power loss in that time after a BAD restores the value the owner disowned. The contract allows it (the change is still on disk within 1 s), and section 12 says so.
- Suspend: contract 3.4 forbids it. Across one, a deferred write waits out the suspend plus what is left of its K checks, since checks do not run while the unit sleeps.
- After a flap, a restart restores the last state on file, at most 1 s old: the value after GOOD, nothing (BAD, NOT_INIT) after BAD, as D-45 says.
- Tests: `ChecksBetweenWrites.AreTheBoundLessTheBudgetOverThePeriodLessOne` (K at 50, 250, 400 ms and above, a period not positive) and `...LeaveTheWriteBudgetOfTheSecond` ((K + 1) · P ≤ 800 ms); `Rewrite.AFlappingRetainNowTagWritesAtMostOnceInKChecks` (a flap at every check for a simulated minute: at most 120 files, 240 without the rule); `Rewrite.AChangeIsHandedOverWithinTheBoundAtLateChecksOf250Ms`, `...Of50Ms` and `...Of400Ms` (checks late by up to P − 1 ns in three patterns, the reviewer's probe among them: every change handed over within K · P + P; all three fail with the ns rule of the first form); `Rewrite.AWriteTheGateDeferredIsWrittenAtTheFlush` and `Rewrite.AFinalFileIsNotHeldBackByAWriteJustBeforeIt` (both fail if the gate is applied there); `Keeper.ALateRetainNowWriteIsReportedOnceUntilOneIsInTime` (a slow-fsync seam, `RetainFileOps::now_mono_ns`); `Keeper.AWriteInFlightAndTheNextAreEachMeasuredOnTheirOwnResult` (F1: a write held in flight at K = 1 while the next is handed over; fails if only the newest result is measured); `WaitUntil.AnInterruptedWaitWaitsAgainUntilTheDeadline` and four more (F2, `libs/scada-tagd/tests/wait_test.cpp`); `ReportLog.ALateRetainNowWriteIsAWarningWithItsMessageId`; `Rewrite.ARestartAfterAFlapThatEndsBadRestoresNothing` and `...EndsGoodRestoresTheValue`; `TagSetValidate.RefusesASlowCheckWhenATagIsRetainNow` (400 and 401; the message names the tag). Five Keeper tests that wrote twice within two checks now wait out the K = 2 checks (`round_after_the_gate`, which asserts that the check between writes nothing; after a FINAL file, one check more), as the rule requires; one of them (`MovedKeeper.TakenValuesKeepTheirFileAndFollowTheRuleOfAttach`) jumped a retain interval in one check, where scada-tagd makes 20.
- Contract 0.24 (section 12, section 18 tests 16 and 25, the change log; migration note).

## D-63: No process-wide queue counts in `scada_status`
Date: 2026-11-16
Phase / issue: 1, #21
Status: accepted by the committee of the lead session on 2026-11-16

Context: D-40 left open for contract 1.0 (P1-13) whether `scada_status` gets process-wide queue counts. Contract section 16 lists it as an open point to settle before 1.0.
Options: add process-wide counts to `scada_status`; keep counts per queue in `scada_queue_stats` only.
Decision: the choice of D-40 is kept. No process-wide queue counts are added; `scada_queue_stats` reports the counts of one side of one queue, and `scada_status` stays 32 bytes. Contract 0.25 marks the point of section 16 settled.
