  - A log record's REASON, like every field value, is cut to the logger's `LOG_MAX_VALUE_BYTES`, 96 bytes, at a UTF-8 boundary: a long tag name in a start failure is cut there.

Reason: the counters the engine and the scheduler already have, published by the thread that owns the tags, with the fewest writes that meet the rate rule of 11.1.

Consequences:
- The engine counts per device (`ChannelEngine::device_counts`, `response_ms`, `points_absent`, `requests_per_s`) and tells its sink of the link (`TagSink::channel_link`). The worker publishes through `SystemTags`.
- A tag set without these tags no longer starts a driver. `scada-conf` does not yet generate them (CS 12.5); the integration tests' tag sets list them.
- Tests:
  - `channel_engine_test.cpp` covers the counts for an answering device, a silent one, busy and gateway answers, an absent answer and a broken one, and the link callbacks.
  - `diagnostics_test.cpp` covers the two windows, a steady 100 requests a second among them.
  - `system_tags_test.cpp` drives `SystemTags` and `ProcessTags` on the fake clock, with the tag write replaced by a recorder (review M2). It covers:
    - a counter written at most once a second;
    - a state written in the step that changed it;
    - no rewrite of an unchanged value;
    - the due time;
    - a stop between two counter writes;
    - the states the stop leaves and `config_state`;
    - the rounding of `response_ms` and of `period.<group>`, and that a tenth is the double nearest it;
    - a stop while `pacing_limited` is true;
    - a refused write counted and written again when next due.
  - `scada-driver-process-integration-test` covers:
    - the `sys.proc` tags after READY;
    - a channel on the main thread's owner, and a tag set without the process owner, refused;
    - a running driver: a connected channel with one live and one disabled device, and every state after the stop;
    - a refused connect;
    - a connect that waits, read as 1 right after a notify of the driver (S4);
    - the values written before READY=1, with a first connect that waits;
    - a dead device's time-outs and demotion beside a live one;
    - a killed driver's tags OWNER_DOWN, and the next life counting from 0;
    - a disabled channel's values;
    - a channel without devices, owned through its state tag;
    - `response_ms` within the time-out and in tenths;
    - the unbuilt device tags NOT_INIT;
    - the state after a refused connect, 0;
    - the walk of 9.1 for the process owner: a tag of that owner that the driver never writes, left GOOD by a previous life, reads NOT_INIT once the driver is ready (review M8);
    - each refused-write path, by `--refuse-write`: a refused `sys.proc` write at the start fails it; one while running is logged; a disabled channel's is logged; a worker's at its stop and the main thread's at the stop are logged once every worker returned.

    The start fails for each of these tag sets: one without the system tags; a channel tag of another type; a missing `sys.proc` tag; a mistyped `sys.dev` tag; a mistyped `period` tag; and the system tags of another owner (S3).
  - The rate rule:
    - after the stop, `requests` equals the requests the simulator received, the stop coming while the tag is behind it;
    - a device's `state` is written in the step of its first answer, within 500 ms of its point;
    - a counter is written within 1.5 s of that answer, though the slow group polls every 10 s and the connect timeout of 5 s caps each wait at 5 s;
    - in the fast group, three writes of `requests` in a row are at least 900 ms apart.

    The fixture's tag sets list every system tag the validator generates for a device without identity checks or refreshed outputs, and the owner `drv-test` (`driver_fixture.h`). Every channel's owner is `drv-test.<channel>`, so every process test also runs with a process owner that no channel writes.
  - The process tests pass run as root and as an unprivileged user (uid 65534, `runuser -u nobody`): 66 of 66 each way.
  - Mutants, each put in by hand, each failing at least one of these tests:
    - counters written every step;
    - `PERIOD_NS` 100 ms or 0;
    - no wake at the due time;
    - states written only at the second;
    - unchanged values rewritten;
    - no counter write at the stop;
    - no stop states;
    - no `config_state` 0 at the stop;
    - CONNECTING written without its notify;
    - no write before READY;
    - the system tags' owner check off;
    - the refused-write start check off;
    - the running refused-write log off;
    - a disabled channel's refused count not stored;
    - a worker's refused count at the stop not stored;
    - the stop's refused-write log not forced;
    - the rounding by `round(x / 0.1) * 0.1`;
    - `period.<group>` not rounded;
    - no `pacing_limited` false at the stop;
    - no walk of 9.1 for the process owner.
- Not tested, and why:
  - A demoted device's recovery has no process test. The simulator cannot make a silent unit answer again; the engine's unit tests cover it.

## D-119: Retained values, logging and alarms of the tags (stage 5)
Date: 2026-11-27
Phase / issue: 2, #100 (P2-03), slice 7c
Status: proposed

Context: contract 5.2, 6 and 8 give rules on the keys of a tag (CF-505 to CF-509, warning CW-510), on logging (CF-801 to CF-803) and on alarms (CF-811 to CF-815). Each needs the tags of the expanded tree (D-116), or several files at once, so they are rules of stage 5. Some points are left open, below.
Options: for each open point, the simplest reading of the contract, or a question on #136 where it does not decide.
Decision: `check_tag_rules` (`src/tag_rules.cpp`) runs after the references (D-116), so it reads the derived type of a virtual tag (D-117). A tag of these rules is a point that has a tag (not `tag: false`), a bit given as a map, or a tag of a tag file; a bit given by its name only has no key these rules read. A name given twice (CF-302, D-116) is checked at the definition the tags hold only, not against its type (S1 of the review of #172). A device given twice (CF-303) is checked once, by its name, also past CF-1001, where the tags do not record every device (S2). Past `limits.max_tags` (CF-1001), the rules that read a tag's value type are not checked: CF-507, CF-803, CF-814 and CF-815; nor CF-505 of an alarm, whose tag may then be one that does not exist (S2). The others are. An error of a template's point is at its instance, with the template note, and one cause at many instances is one error (D-114).
- **Retained values (5.2):**
  - a tag is retained when `retain` is `true`, or when a manual tag gives no `retain` (5.1). CF-507 `string tags cannot be retained` is at `retain`, else at the manual tag's `value_type`. The message is section 10's exactly: a diagnostic has no place for a note outside the fixed message (its notes are the places of a template, 1.4), so that the default is the cause is not said; the wording is point 25 on #136.
  - CF-506 for `retain_now: true` and for any `retain_max_age` on a tag that is not retained. `retain_now: false` needs nothing.
  - CF-509 for any `retain_now`, `true` or `false`, on a virtual tag, and then no CF-506. A point has no `retain_now` key: the schema refuses it at stage 3, so CF-509 "on a point tag" is never reached (on #136).
  - CF-508 for `retain_max_age` on a tag of `kind: counter`, a point's or a tag file's. The other tags 5.2 lists are generated (12.5) and have no key a user writes, so CF-508 cannot reach them. Conformance test 10 has an output mode tag "through an override", which no key of the schemas gives (on #136).
  - CW-510 `retain_max_age has no effect until the tag database supports it (#89)` for every `retain_max_age`. On a template's point it is given once, at the first instance met, without the "(and N more instances)" of an error (S3 of the review of #172): the grouping of D-114 keeps its diagnostics in `GroupedErrors`, which counts one past its kept limit as an error, so a warning there could refuse a tree. A warning of one template line at many instances is one warning, and says nothing more.
- **Logging (6):**
  - the settings of a tag are: its `logging`; without it, `default_policy`; `none` logs nothing. A map with `policy` takes the policy and overrides `mode` and `deadband`. A map without `policy` must give `mode` and `raw_retention`: one CF-802 for each it lacks, at the `logging` map. A policy name that does not exist is CF-310 of the references, and nothing more.
  - CF-803 `string tag {name} can be logged on change only` when the mode of those settings is `periodic` or `both`. It is at the tag's `logging`, or at its name when the mode comes from `default_policy`. So a periodic `default_policy` refuses every STRING tag without `logging` (on #136).
  - CF-801 compares each tier of `aggregate_tiers` with the one before: `its interval must be longer than that of {tier}` when it is not longer, else `its interval must be a whole multiple of that of {tier}`. The error is at the interval. A tier whose interval the schema refused is skipped, and the next tier is not compared with it; stage 5 never meets one through validation, and a unit test calls the check alone for it.
- **Percentages (2.2):** CF-505 `{name}: a percentage needs range` for a percentage `deadband` written in the tag's own alarm or `logging` map, on a tag that gives no `range`, at the deadband. A percentage that comes from a named policy or from `default_policy` gives no CF-505 (the lead's ruling M1 of the review of #172: otherwise the examples of 5.1 with 6, which conformance test 1 needs valid, the `flow_meter_x` example of reference 12.3 and every BOOL, bit and STRING tag under such a default policy were refused). What such a percentage means at run time on a tag without `range` is on #136. Only a point and a tag of a tag file have `range`; a bit, a generated tag and a STRING tag have none. Corpus case `s5-valid-logging-policies` holds those examples.
- **Alarms (8):**
  - the alarms are those of a point, a bit, a tag of a tag file and `alarms/*.yaml`. The tag of an alarm is its `tag`, else the tag it is written in; an expression alarm is on no tag and is not checked here. An alarm on a tag that does not exist is CF-310 of the references (D-116), and nothing more.
  - CF-811 `{key} is given in {a} and {b}` at the key of each later alarms file, in path order, naming the first file that gives `priorities`, `max_shelve` or `classes`.
  - CF-812 `tag {name} has two alarms with id {id}` at each alarm after the first of an id on a tag, at its `id`, else at the alarm. The id defaults to the type. The alarms are taken in the order of the expanded tree (channels, devices, points, bits), then of the files in path order. The state tags of such alarms are made once and are not CF-302 (D-116).
  - CF-813 `tag {name}: {a} must be above {b}`, with `{a}` and `{b}` the ids. Each `high_high` alarm whose limit is a number is compared with the highest numeric `high` limit of its tag, and each `low` with the highest numeric `low_low`. Two integer limits are compared exactly, as int64 or uint64; a limit with a decimal point or an exponent makes the comparison binary64 (M2 of the review of #172). An equal limit is not above it. The error is at the limit of the `high_high` or `low` alarm. One error per alarm, however many the others: the check is linear in the alarms of a tag. A limit given as a tag is not compared.
  - CF-814 `string tag {name} cannot have alarms` for any alarm on a STRING tag, at the alarm.
  - CF-815 `BOOL tag {name} cannot have a {type} alarm` for the four limit types, `high_high`, `high`, `low` and `low_low` (8: "the four limit types"), at the `type`. `deviation` and `rate_of_change` also take a `limit`; on a BOOL tag they are not refused here (on #136).

Consequences: the warnings CW-804 (retention classes) and CW-805 (an interval finer than the refresh) are not in this slice. CW-805 reads the poll groups and the tasks, which slice 7d has. `Reader` and `Reporter` move from `src/tag_names.cpp` to `src/tree_reader.h`, in `scada::conf::detail`, and are shared, unchanged except for `Reporter::warning`. Measured in release on x86-64, on 2 instances of a 20,000-point `repeat`, each point with `range`, `retain`, a logging policy and four limit alarms: 1.69 s, against 1.42 s without the check. Peak memory, measured by the C++ reviewer on 140,000 tags, goes from about 285 MB to 325 MB (S7 of the review of #172), within the bound of D-114.
Not changed after the review of #172, as noted there: two expression alarms of one `name` and `id` are CF-302 of their state tags (D-116), not CF-812, since an expression alarm is on no tag; CF-813 names the alarms by their ids; an alarm of a template's point with an absolute `tag:` is on that one tag at every instance, so each instance after the first gives its own CF-812: the cause is the template's line, but the tag is the same, and the error says which instance made the second alarm.
